Mean original-time error across the cue's three sample positions on the two smooth clocks grew from .096 of the phrase for fixed timing to .188 and .176 for live alignment. Whole-phrase consistency poorly localized the cue when different parts moved in different directions. Neither voice met the combined retention and transfer criteria.

The two voices each contribute 32 fresh locally synthesized utterances; timing variants are feature-space transforms. The external listener used the stored training trajectories without their labels for clock selection; the registered connected decoder remained a separate comparator. Independently reconstructed all 320 alignments, nearest-template choices and signed decisions for every candidate from WAV-derived features using linear interpolation and distance differences. External cue_clock.py VOICE, cue_clock_check.py and cue_clock_local.py via mac.sh reproduce the saved decisions, scores and evaluator-only comparisons. No default changed.

### Local landmarks for the internal number ending

Freeze the prior classifier and nine alignment candidates. Use mean training acoustics at the two samples on each side of the frozen three-sample window. Compare (i) minimum local discrepancy, (ii) local discrepancy plus a learned identity-clock preference, (iii) distant samples only with the same fitting opportunity, (iv) previous whole-context policy, (v) identity and (vi) registered four-part decoder. Normalize candidate discrepancy by its across-candidate mean, then add lambda times absolute log2 exponent. Select lambda in 0,.05,.15,.4,1,3 by least mean absolute cue-location error using old 199-wpm teaching phrases with leave-one-stem-out centroids and generated timing interventions: identity twice, powers .8 and 1.25, smooth perturbations ±.08 sine. No number labels used to fit the locator; simulated source timing is supervision. Primary fresh 221-wpm test uses held eight stems in cyclic offset one, both endings crossed, transformations identity, .70/1.45 powers, ±.12 sine; never expose transform to locator. Per voice require +6/64 smooth-clock first endings over each of fixed and previous whole-context policy, ≤2 clean losses vs fixed and ≤3 power-clock losses vs previous whole-context. Report membership of both halves of each ending pair, comparisons with fitted distant context, signed changes and sampling uncertainty. Independently test timing localization, including swapping selected alignments between opposite distortions.

Both local fits chose lambda 3 and kept identity on all 160 hearings per voice. To distinguish weak local features from mismatched reference material, run a separate privileged-reference diagnostic on the same hearings, with no identity penalty. Compare unlabelled clean reference phrases from the same test voice: (a) same two nouns and final ending but opposite first ending, and (b) cyclically next noun pair with the same opposing first ending and final ending. Use only the four local reference positions for alignment, never its classifier-window samples or number label. These references require additional acoustic material and evaluator-selected pairings. Compare realized accuracy and geometric timing errors to the original population-mean context.

Clean / power / smooth first-ending counts (denominators 32/64/64): Samantha fitted local and fixed 30/37/44, unpenalized local 23/44/34, fitted distant 30/37/44, previous whole context 23/49/33, registered decoder 24/48/42; Joelle fitted local and fixed 29/34/40, unpenalized local 20/38/41, fitted distant 29/34/40, previous whole context 24/46/38, registered 22/49/40. Fitted local complete pairs: 14/16, 5/32, 12/32 Samantha and 13/16, 3/32, 8/32 Joelle. Swapping the chosen clock produced identical results because every selection was identity. Smooth net vs whole context +11 [2,20] and +2 [−6,12], but zero vs fixed; power loss −12 [−18,−5] and −12 [−22,−2]. Intervals resample eight noun pairs. Both missed the primary. No selective realignment demonstrated.

Reference diagnostic: matching local samples from the same lexical context with an opposing first ending vs another pair improved mean cue-position error on power (.079 vs .148, .061 vs .136) and smooth (.093 vs .135, .079 vs .143) for Samantha and Joelle respectively, in units of phrase duration. Smooth accuracy same/other was 41/34 and 42/31, power 44/38 and 52/34. Even this matched context on clean speech scored only 21/32 and 26/32 vs fixed 30/32 and 29/32. The reference thus contains useful landmarks, but the alignment can also move when only the ending changes. The matched extra reference was supplied in this diagnostic and unavailable in the primary evaluation. Verify with ending_landmark.py VOICE fit/test, ending_landmark_check.py and ending_landmark_ref.py via mac.sh in the external dual-role-word archive. Recomputed the 320 held cost vectors from locally synthesized recordings, including nearest preceding/following samples and training means. Only 32 distinct test recordings per voice; distortions act on acoustic features. No deployment change.

### Retrieving an unlabelled acoustic memory to time a spoken inflection

Freeze one chronological archive of eight 201-wpm recordings per voice, one per held noun pair at cyclic offset three, first ending i modulo two and final ending floor(i/2) modulo two. Archive both noun combinations and their pronunciation but supply no labels, handles or test feedback to retrieval. Render all four target endings at 235 wpm separately. Only one quarter repeat archival lexical content, all target recordings are new. Use mean squared acoustic distance on 48 reference samples for monotone dynamic time warping with diagonal, horizontal, vertical steps and .15 feature-cost penalty per non-diagonal step. Omit reference samples falling within the frozen first-ending window (including half-sample margins); linearly interpolate alignment between adjacent visible anchors. Rank eight references by total path cost, map the three cue times and select the nearest of the frozen nine clocks. Compare retrieval, forced same pair, cyclic reassignment of the selected reference, fixed clock, previous whole-context alignment and registered connected decoder. Freeze primary endpoints: ≥75% correct noun-pair retrieval including when first ending changes, ≥5/64 more smooth-clock correct than fixed and than reassigned reference, ≤2 clean losses vs fixed, independently per voice. Report paired power/smooth outcomes, correct pair vs accuracy under changed endings, alternative when the right reference is withheld and reverse-order invariance. The assigned clock family and acoustic classifier are supplied. This tests an external acoustic store; storage is eight reference feature arrays and retrieval work counts even on clean cases.

Opened audit: every incorrect retrieval chose a reference whose final ending matched the query (73/73 Samantha, 58/58 Joelle). Same-pair retrieval was 80/80 and 79/80 if the final ending matched the archived one, only 7/80 and 23/80 if it differed. Diagnostic on this opened panel: exclude the last third of the reference trajectory from retrieval cost as well as the earlier cue mask, keeping the original per-reference clocks exactly fixed. Compare changed-final vs unchanged-final success and subsequent first-ending choices. This positional mask is evaluator-designed after inspecting these failures.

Primary acoustic retrieval recovered the noun pair on 87/160 and 102/160 hearings. On changed first endings: 40/80 and 41/80 correct retrievals, with 53/80 and 47/80 correct first-number decisions. For clean / power / smooth clocks, correct first-number choices respectively: Samantha retrieved 25/47/43, forced same pair 27/52/47, reassigned 23/43/41, fixed 27/37/46, whole context 25/48/32, right pair withheld 23/41/39, registered decoder 26/51/44. Joelle retrieved 25/51/42, forced 26/49/47, reassigned 24/41/44, fixed 27/38/44, whole context 21/42/34, withheld 22/41/33, registered 22/38/36. Denominators 32/64/64; complete first-ending pairs for retrieved policy 9/15/12 and 9/19/13 of 16/32/32. Power gains vs fixed +10 [1,18] and +13 [3,23]; smooth −3 [−8,2] and −2 [−15,10]. Intervals resample eight noun pairs. Primary missed retrieval and smooth improvement thresholds. Payload 39,936 bytes per voice. Local median/p95 time for archive alignment and ranking only was 13.4/18.5 ms and 13.0/16.6 ms, measured during concurrent voice evaluations; excludes feature extraction and synthesis.

Post hoc tail masking raised same-pair retrieval with changed final endings to 70/80 and 63/80 while reducing unchanged-final to 78/80 and 71/80. First-ending accuracy clean/power/smooth became 26/51/47 and 23/46/44. Better lexical retrieval helped Samantha overall but hurt Joelle (118→113/160). Even the supplied correct reference attained 47/64 smooth on each voice. Retrieving an appropriate phrase and timing the inflection within it need separate tests. Each voice had eight unlabelled archived renditions and 32 distinct new test recordings; inverse order selected identical references throughout, and SHA-256 hashes of all 40 WAVs were distinct per voice. Independently rebuilt path costs on five hearings per voice × all eight references and checked all stored alternative selections. Reproduce with memory_cue.py VOICE, memory_cue_check.py and memory_cue_tail.py through mac.sh in the external dual-role-word archive. Archive lookup and interpolation are external, the four-part acoustic comparator uses registered scoring. No default or deployment change.

### Learning which acoustic regions matter for a retrieval request

Two anonymous request handles are grounded by demonstrations selecting a recording: match both noun identities or match the final ending. Source and held vocabularies are disjoint. Cross four possible first nouns with two second nouns. Each archived pair has a checkerboard final ending; target queries change that ending. For each query offer the matching noun pair and one other phrase that matches the new final ending; foils share either the first or second noun. The same public recordings and menu thus require opposite selections for the two handles. Use both first endings, counterbalance menu order, and evaluate neutral panels where the matchers can agree separately. No word boundaries, noun IDs or ending bits go into acoustic distances. Distance vector: eight equal time bins, each using the cheapest local time offset of −4 to +4 on a 48-sample grid and a squared discrepancy. Fit two simplex-weighted distances by binary pointer loss with .01 quadratic regularization toward uniform, using only clean, .8 and 1.25 power-clock source examples. Controls: shared uniform, fixed first-two-thirds and final-third rules, exchanged fits, and the best source-selected single bin per request. Freeze before testing 207/233 wpm archived/query rates, held nouns and distortions 1, .70, 1.45, ±.12 sine. Require ≥75% both-request success per voice for each foil relation and ≥10 percentage points over the fixed paired region rules overall. Separately report clean and distorted, transfer to equal-ending menus and bootstrap across lexical pairs. No claim about inferring the spoken request itself: handles and demonstrations are supplied.

The primary noun metric favored early samples; both voices passed the shared-second foil requirement but failed shared-first. Opened-panel diagnostic: fit the identical noun metric separately on source menus with the first noun shared and menus with the second noun shared. Supply the test foil class only to this evaluator and compare with the original single noun metric. This tests whether source supervision specific to the distinction suffices. The ending metric and its evidence remain fixed.

Both voices located the final ending on all 160/160 conflict menus and all 160/160 neutral menus per voice. Ending fits concentrated ≥84% of weight in bin seven of eight. On conflict menus the complete two-request score therefore equaled noun accuracy: Samantha 45/80 when the first noun was shared vs 75/80 when the second was shared, Joelle 42/80 vs 74/80. Clean / power / smooth joint counts Samantha 24/32,48/64,48/64; Joelle 25/32,46/64,45/64. Overall learned / fixed paired regions / independently selected single bins: 120/122/119 of 160 Samantha, 116/102/97 Joelle. Paired gain over fixed regions −1.25 points [−13.75,11.25] and +8.75 [−10.625,30], resampling eight noun pairs. Swapping the two fits yielded 0/160 both voices on conflict menus; the request-independent metric necessarily failed joint conflict but gave 160/160 and 158/160 joint neutral. Learned neutral joint 119/160 and 142/160. Primary failed on both voices. A control had an exact acoustic distance tie; the verifier counts tied decisions (absolute margin ≤1e−12) as unresolved and checks reversal on all decisive menus.

When the evaluator supplied which noun differed, source fits used bins five/six for a differing second noun and one/two for a differing first noun. Shared-first accuracy rose from 45 to 63/80 Samantha and 42 to 51/80 Joelle; shared-second was 74/80 and 75/80. This localizes some loss to using one noun metric regardless of what alternatives share; Joelle still had substantial acoustic errors even given that information. Six source and six held stem identities per voice, 16 source query recordings and 32 held, eight additional archive recordings per split. Each of 96 source menus had two task pointers and reuses recordings across foil choices and timing variants. All forty held WAVs were bytewise distinct and disjoint from the 24 source WAVs within voice. Independently recalculated sampled block distances from waveforms, both target pointers from reference identities, and all paired contrasts from saved margins. The acoustic features were obtained locally, and learned retrieval and pointing ran as external numerical replay with supplied handles. Reproduce retrieval_focus.py VOICE fit/test, retrieval_focus_audio.py, retrieval_focus_check.py and retrieval_focus_relation.py via mac.sh in dual-role-word. No default deployment change.
